Annual Report 2025-261
Notice
NOTICE  IS HEREBY GIVEN THAT the Fifth (5th) Annual General 
Meeting (‘AGM’)  of Sagility Limited (formerly Sagility 
India Limited, earlier Sagility India Private Limited) , (‘the 
Company’) will be held on Thursday, 20th August 2026 at 04:00 
P .M. (IST) through Video Conferencing (‘VC’)/ Other Audio 
Visual Means (‘OAVM’) to transact the following businesses:
ORDINARY BUSINESSES
1. To receive, consider, and adopt the: 
(A) Audited standalone financial statements of the 
Company for the financial year ended 31st March 
2026 together with the reports of the Board of 
Directors and Auditors thereon; and 
(B)  Audited consolidated financial statements of the 
Company for the financial year ended 31st March 
2026 together with the report of Auditors thereon.
 To consider, and if thought fit, pass the following resolution 
as an Ordinary Resolution :
 “RESOLVED THAT  the audited standalone financial 
statements of the Company for the financial year ended 
31st March 2026 together with the reports of the Board 
of Directors and Auditors thereon, as circulated to the 
members, be and are hereby approved and adopted. 
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT  the audited consolidated 
financial statements of the Company for the financial 
year ended 31st March 2026 together with the report of the 
Auditors thereon, as circulated to the members, be and 
are hereby approved and adopted. ”
2. To appoint a director in place of Mr. Hari 
Gopalakrishnan (DIN: 03289463), a Non-Executive 
Non-Independent Director, who retires by rotation 
and being eligible, offers himself for re-appointment. To consider, and if thought fit, pass the following resolution 
as an Ordinary Resolution :
 “RESOLVED THAT  in accordance with the provisions 
of Section 152 and any other applicable provisions of 
the Companies Act, 2013 and Rules made thereunder 
(including any statutory modification(s) and/or re-
enactment(s) thereof, for the time being in force) read with 
Article 110 of the Articles of Association of the Company, 
Mr. Hari Gopalakrishnan (DIN: 03289463), being a Non-
Executive Non-Independent Director, who retires by 
rotation at this ensuing Annual General Meeting of the 
Company, and being eligible, seeks re-appointment, 
be and is hereby re-appointed as a Non-Executive Non-
Independent Director of the Company, liable to retire by 
rotation. ”
3. To confirm the Interim Dividend of ` 0.05 (Five paise 
only) per equity share of ` 10 each paid during the 
year and to declare Final Dividend of ` 0.10 per 
equity share of ` 10 each for the Financial Year ended  
31st March, 2026.
 To consider, and if thought fit, pass the following resolution 
as an Ordinary Resolution :
 “ RESOLVED THAT  Interim Dividend of `0.05 (Five paise 
only) per equity share of ` 10 each fully paid-up, declared 
by the Board of Directors in October 2025 and paid to the 
Members of the Company in November 2025 be and is 
hereby confirmed.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT  approval be and is hereby 
accorded for declaration and payment of final dividend of 
` 0.10 (Ten Paise Only) per equity share of the face value of 
` 10 (Rupees Ten) each fully paid up, of the Company, as 
recommended by the Board of Directors for the financial 
year ended 31st March 2026”
By order of the Board of Directors

Annual Report 2025-262
Sagility LimitedNOTES:
a. Since no special business is proposed to be transacted at 
the Annual General Meeting, the Statement pursuant to 
Section 102 of the Companies Act, 2013 is not required. 
However, the disclosures pertaining to Item No. 2 of the 
Notice, as required under the Securities and Exchange 
Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI Listing 
Regulations”) and Secretarial Standard-2 on General 
Meetings issued by the Institute of Company Secretaries 
of India (“SS-2”), form an integral part of this Notice.
b. Ministry of Corporate Affairs (“MCA”) vide its General 
Circular No. 03/2025 dated 22nd September 2025 read with 
circulars issued earlier on the subject (“MCA Circulars”), 
have permitted to conduct the Annual General Meeting 
(“AGM”) virtually, without physical presence of Members 
at a common venue.
 In compliance with the MCA Circulars, the provisions of 
the Act and the SEBI Listing Regulations, the 5th AGM of the 
Company is being held virtually and transcript of the same 
shall be made available on the website of the Company.
 MUFG Intime India Private Limited (MUFG) will be 
providing the facilities for participation in the AGM through 
VC/OAVM, e-voting during the AGM and remote e-voting.
c. The deemed venue for the AGM shall be the Registered 
Office of the Company and since this AGM is held 
through Video Conference/Other Audio Visual Means 
(“VC/OAVM”), route map to the venue is not required and 
therefore, the same is not annexed to this Notice. 
d. Members attending the meeting through VC/OAVM shall 
be reckoned for the purpose of quorum under Section 103 
of the Act. Members holding equity shares as on Thursday, 
13th August 2026 (“Cut-off date”) shall be eligible to attend 
and vote on resolutions proposed at the AGM. Any person 
who is not a Member as on the Cut-off date shall treat this 
Notice for informational purpose only.
e. Attendance through VC/OAVM is restricted and hence, 
Members shall be eligible to join the meeting on first-
come-first-serve basis. However, attendance of Members 
holding more than 2% of the paid-up equity share 
capital, Institutional investors, Directors, Key Managerial 
Personnel, Promoters, and Auditors, if any, will not be 
restricted on first- come-first-serve basis.
 Members may join the AGM before 30 Minutes of the 
scheduled time by following the procedure outlined in the 
‘Instameet VC Instructions for shareholders’ .
f. Since the 5th AGM is being held through VC/OAVM in 
accordance with the MCA Circulars, physical attendance 
of Members has been dispensed with. Accordingly, the 
facility of appointment of proxy would not be available to the Members for attending the 5th AGM, and therefore, 
proxy form and attendance slip are not annexed to this 
Notice.
g. In compliance with Section 108 of the Act, read with 
the corresponding rules, Regulation 44 of the LODR 
Regulations and relevant SEBI circulars, the Company 
has provided a remote e-voting facility to its members 
in respect of the business to be transacted at the AGM. 
The facility for e-voting during the AGM will also be made 
available for 15 minutes after the conclusion of the AGM. 
Members present in the AGM through VC/OAVM and 
who have not cast their vote on the resolutions through 
remote e-voting and are otherwise not barred from doing 
so, shall be eligible to vote through the e-voting system 
during the AGM. Members who have cast their votes by 
remote e-voting prior to the AGM may participate in the 
AGM but shall not be entitled to cast their votes again. 
The manner of voting remotely by members holding 
shares in dematerialized mode and for members who 
have not registered their email addresses is provided 
in the ‘Instructions for e-voting’ and the manner for 
e-voting during the AGM is provided under ‘Instameet VC 
Instructions for Shareholders’ which forms part of this 
Notice. Members are requested to carefully read the 
instructions in connection with remote e-voting as 
appended to this Notice.
h. The Board has appointed Mr. Rupesh Agarwal, 
Managing Partner, Chandrasekaran Associates, 
Company Secretaries (FRN: P1988DE002500; Peer 
Review Certificate No.: 6689/2025), in his absence  
Mr. Shashikant Tiwari, Partner, Chandrasekaran 
Associates, in his absence Mr. Lakhan Gupta, Partner, 
Chandrasekaran Associates, Company Secretaries, as 
the scrutinizer (“Scrutinizer”) for conducting the e-voting 
process in a fair and transparent manner.
i. The remote e-voting period commences on Monday, 17th 
August 2026 (09:00 a.m. IST) and ends on Wednesday, 
19th August 2026 (05:00 p.m. IST). The e-voting module 
will be disabled by MUFG thereafter. Members will not 
be allowed to vote again on any resolution on which vote 
has already been cast. The voting rights of members 
shall be proportionate to their share of the paid-up equity 
share capital of the Company as on the cut-off date, i.e. 
Thursday, 13th August 2026.
j. Corporate shareholders/institutional shareholders 
intending to authorize their representative(s) to attend 
/ vote at the 5th AGM are requested to send from their 
registered e-mail address, scan copy of the relevant 
Board Resolution/ Authority Letter, etc. authorizing 
their representative(s) to attend / vote, to the Scrutinizer 
on e-mail ID at rupesh@cacsindia.com  with a copy 
marked to RTA e-mail enotices@in.mpms.mufg.com  and 
investorservices@sagility.com .

n. Members ar e requested to note that SEBI, vide the 
Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) (Fifth Amendment) Regulations, 2025, has amended Regulation 12 of the SEBI Listing Regulations with effect from 8
th November 2025. 
Pursuant to the said amendment, dividends are required to be paid only through electronic modes of payment, and the issuance of dividend warrants or cheques has been discontinued.
o.   Dividend income is taxable in the hands of Members and

Name of the Director  Hari Gopalakrishnan
Directors Identification Number 03289463 
Date of Birth (Age in years) 22nd March 1977; 49 years 
Original date of appointment 28th July 2021
Qualifications He holds a bachelor’s degree in medicine and surgery from University of 
Kerala and post-graduate diploma in management from Indian Institute of 
Management, Ahmedabad.
Experience and expertise in specific functional 
area He has more than 16 years of experience and has been associated with Baring 
Private Equity Asia (BPEA) since 2007 (founding member of the BPEA India 
team) and is currently serving as a partner and global co-head of services, 
private capital in the BPEA EQT team. He is also a member of EQT’s Fund’s 
Investment Committee, Exits and Liquidity Committee and the Mid Market 
Investment Committee and focusses on investments in the technology and 
healthcare sectors.
He holds expertise across several functional areas such as BPM, US-based 
healthcare payer-provider, global professional services, finance and accounting, 
human capital management, risk management and strategic transactions..
Shareholding in the Company including beneficial 
holdingNil 
Terms and conditions of appointment or re-
appointmentHe was appointed with effect from 28th July 2021 as a First Director of the 
Company. Post‑IPO, the Board, at its meeting held on 24th June 2024, noted 
the continuation of this appointment as a Non‑Executive Non‑Independent 
Director (liable to retire by rotation). 
In accordance with Section 152 of the Companies Act, 2013, he retired by 
rotation and was re-appointed as director at the Annual General Meeting held 
on 25th September 2024.

Brief Resume of the Director Hari Gopalakrishnan has more than 16 years of experience and has been 
associated with Baring Private Equity Asia (BPEA) since 2007 (founding member 
of the BPEA India team) and is currently serving as a partner and global co-head 
of services, private capital in the BPEA EQT team. He is also a member of EQT’s 
Fund’s Investment Committee, Exits and Liquidity Committee and the Mid 
Market Investment Committee and focusses on investments in the technology 
and healthcare sectors.
Prior to joining BPEA, Hari was with an India focused investment fund, New 
Vernon Capital, in their Mumbai office. He holds a bachelor’s degree in 
medicine and surgery from University of Kerala and post-graduate diploma in 
management from Indian Institute of Management, Ahmedabad.

In terms of Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) General Circular 
No. 03/2025 dated 22nd September 2025, the companies can 
continue to conduct AGMs by VC or OAVM, as per the existing procedural requirements. Till further orders, the relaxations will 
remain in force.
Shareholders are advised to update their mobile number and 
email Id correctly in their demat accounts to access InstaMeet 
facility. 
Login method for shareholders to attend the General 
Meeting through InstaMeet: 
a) Visi t URL: https://instameet.in.mpms.mufg.com  & click 
on “Login”. 
b) S elect the “Company Name” and register with your 
following details: 
c) S elect Check Box - Demat Account No. / Folio No. / PAN
• Shareholders holding shares in NSDL/ CDSL demat 
account shall select check box - Demat Account No. 
and enter the 16-digit demat account number .
• Shareholders shall select check box – PAN  and 
enter 10-digit Permanent Account Number (PAN). 
Shareholders who have not updated their PAN with 
the Depository Participant (DP)/ Company shall use 
the sequence number provided by MUFG Intime, if 
applicable.
• Mobile No: Mobile No. as updated with DP is 
displayed automatically. Shareholders who have not 
updated their Mobile No with the DP shall enter the 
mobile no.
• Email ID: Email Id as updated with DP is displayed 
automatically. Shareholders who have not updated 
their Email Id with the DP shall enter the Email Id.
d) Clic k “Go to Meeting” .
 Y ou are now registered for InstaMeet, and your attendance 
is marked for the meeting.
Instructions for shareholders to Speak during the General Meeting through InstaMeet:
a) Shar eholders who would like to speak during the 
meeting must register their request with the Company at 
investorservices@sagility.com. 
b) Shar eholders will get confirmation on first cum first basis 
depending upon the provision made by the Company. 
c) Shar eholders will receive “speaking serial number” once 
they mark attendance for the meeting. Please remember speaking serial number and start your conversation with 
panellist by switching on video mode and audio of your 
device.
d) Other shar eholder who has not registered as “Speaker 
Shareholder” may still ask questions to the panellist via 
active chat-board during the meeting.
 *Shar eholders are requested to speak only when 
moderator of the meeting/ management will announce 
the name and serial number for speaking. Instructions for Shareholders to Vote during the General 
Meeting through InstaMeet:
Once the electronic voting is activated during the meeting, 
shareholders who have not exercised their vote through the remote e-voting can cast the vote as under:
a) On the Shareholders VC page, click on link “Cast your 
vote” .
b) Ent er your 16-digit Demat Account No. / Folio No. and OTP 
(received on the registered mobile number/ registered email Id) received during registration for InstaMeet. 
c) Clic k on ‘Submit’ . 
d) Af ter successful login, you will see “Resolution 
Description” and against the same the option “Favour/ 
Against” for voting. 
e) Cas t your vote by selecting appropriate option i.e. “Favour/
Against” as desired. Enter the number of shares (which 
represents no. of votes) as on the cut-off date under 
‘Favour/Against’ .
f) Af ter selecting the appropriate option i.e. Favour/Against 
as desired and you have decided to vote, click on “Save” .  
A confirmation box will be displayed. If you wish to confirm your vote, click on “Confirm” , else to change 
your vote, click on “Back” and accordingly modify your 
vote. Once you confirm your vote on the resolution, 
you will not be allowed to modify or change your vote 
subsequently.
Note: 
Shareholders/ Members, who will be present in the General 
Meeting through InstaMeet facility and have not casted their 
vote on the Resolutions through remote e-Voting and are 
otherwise not barred from doing so, shall be eligible to vote 
through e-Voting facility during the meeting. 
Shareholders/ Members who have voted through Remote 
e-Voting prior to the General Meeting will be eligible to attend/ 
participate in the General Meeting through InstaMeet. However, 
they will not be eligible to vote again during the meeting. 
Shareholders/ Members are encouraged to join the Meeting 
through Tablets/ Laptops connected through broadband for 
better experience.
Shareholders/ Members are required to use Internet with a 
good speed (preferably 2 MBPS download stream) to avoid any 
disturbance during the meeting.
Please note that Shareholders/ Members connecting from 
Mobile Devices or Tablets or through Laptops connecting 
via Mobile Hotspot may experience Audio/Visual loss due to 
fluctuation in their network. It is therefore recommended to use 
stable Wi-FI or LAN connection to mitigate any kind of aforesaid 
glitches.

REMOTE E-VOTING INSTRUCTIONS FOR 
SHAREHOLDERS
In terms of SEBI circular no. HO/49/14/14(7)2025-CFD-
POD2/I/3762/2026 dated January 30, 2026, Individual 
shareholders holding securities in demat mode are allowed to 
vote through their demat account maintained with Depositories and Depository Participants. 
Shareholders are advised to update their mobile number and 
email Id correctly in their demat accounts to access remote 
e-Voting facility. 
Login method for Individual shareholders holding 
securities in demat mode:
Individual Shareholders holding securities in demat mode with 
NSDL
METHOD 1 - NSDL OTP based login
a) Visit URL: https://eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/
evoting/evotinglogin.jsp  
b) Ent er your 8 - character DP ID, 8 - digit Client Id, PAN, 
Verification code and generate OTP . 
c) Ent er the OTP received on your registered email ID/ mobile 
number and click on login. 
d) P ost successful authentication, you will be re-directed to 
NSDL depository website wherein you will be able to see 
e-Voting services under Value added services. Click on 
“Access to e-Voting” under e-Voting services. 
e) Clic k on “MUFG InTime” or “evoting link displayed 
alongside Company’s Name” and you will be redirected to 
InstaVote website for casting the vote during the remote 
e-voting period. 
METHOD 2 - NSDL IDeAS facility 
Shareholders registered for IDeAS facility: 
a) Visi t URL: https://eservices.nsdl.com and click on 
“Beneficial Owner” icon under “IDeAS Login Section” .
b) Enter IDeAS User ID, Password, Verification code & click 
on “Log-in” .
c) P ost successful authentication, you will be able to see 
e-Voting services under Value added services section. Click on “Access to e-Voting” under e-Voting services. 
d) Clic k on “MUFG InTime” or “evoting link displayed 
alongside Company’s Name” and you will be redirected to InstaVote website for casting the vote during the remote 
e-voting period. 
Shareholders not registered for IDeAS facility: 
a) T o register, visit URL: https://eservices.nsdl.com and 
select “Register Online for IDeAS Portal” or click on https://
eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/IdeasDirectReg.jsp 
Shareholders/ Members can also download NSDL 
Mobile App “NSDL Speede” facility by scanning the QR 
code mentioned below for seamless voting experience  
b) Ent er 8-character DP ID, 8-digit Client ID, Mobile no, 
Verification code & click on “Submit” .
c) Ent er the last 4 digits of your bank account / generate 
‘OTP’

STEP 3 – Steps to cast vote for Resolutions through InstaVote
The corporate shareholder can vote by two methods, during the 
remote e-voting period. 
METHOD 1 - VOTES ENTRY 
a) Visit URL: https://instavote.linkintime.co.in  and login with 
InstaVote Login credentials. 
b) Click on “Votes Entry” tab under the Menu section. 
c) Enter the “Event No. ” for which you want to cast vote. 
 Event No. can be viewed on the home page of InstaVote 
under “On-going Events” . 
d) Enter “16-digit Demat Account No. ” .
e) Refer the Resolution description and cast your vote by 
selecting your desired option ‘Favour / Against’ (If you wish 
to view the entire Resolution details, click on the ‘View 
Resolution’ file link). After selecting the desired option i.e. 
Favour / Against, click on ‘Submit’ . 
f) A confirmation box will be displayed. If you wish to confirm 
your vote, click on ‘Yes’ , else to change your vote, click on 
‘No’ and accordingly modify your vote. 
(Once you cast your vote on the resolution, you will not be 
allowed to modify or change it subsequently).
METHOD 2 - VOTES UPLOAD
a) Visit URL: https://instavote.linkintime.co.in  and login with 
InstaVote Login credentials.
b) After successful login, you will see “Notification for 
e-voting” . 
c) Select “View” icon for “Company’s Name / Event number” . 
d) E-voting page will appear. 
e) Download sample vote file from “Download Sample Vote 
File” tab. 
f) Cast your vote by selecting your desired option ‘Favour / 
Against’ in the sample vote file and upload the same under 
“Upload Vote File” option. 
g) Click on ‘Submit’ . ‘Data uploaded successfully’ message 
will be displayed. 
(Once you cast your vote on the resolution, you will not be 
allowed to modify or change it subsequently).

Transforming 
Healthcare Operations Through Decision Intelligence
Annual Report 2025-26
2
Sagility Limited
Annual Report 2025-26 2
=== page 18 ===
Rising medical costs, regulatory complexity, 
and fragmented data and systems are placing growing pressure on interconnected workflows across the healthcare lifecycle, including enrollment, claims, clinical operations, payment integrity, member and provider engagement, and revenue cycle management. We believe the rapid acceleration and deployment of technology and AI are the key to harnessing operational efficiency and outcomes, unlocking the value that these pressures impede.
The industry is therefore shifting from 
managing transactions to orchestrating outcomes. This requires the ability to connect fragmented data, apply healthcare domain expertise, generate actionable intelligence, and translate decisions into consistent execution across complex operating environments.
Sagility is uniquely positioned to play this role 
as the Decision Intelligence and Outcomes 
layer for healthcare operations. With deep specialization in U.S. healthcare, scaled operational execution, and embedded technology, analytics, and AI capabilities, Sagility helps clients move from fragmented workflows to integrated, intelligence-led operating models that are designed around measurable business, clinical, and experience outcomes.
This role comes to life by embedding 
intelligence directly into the flow of work. Sagility helps convert operational data into clearer decisions, supports faster and more accurate execution, and creates feedback loops that continuously improve performance across the healthcare lifecycle. The result is an operating model that connects insight, action, and accountability.
As healthcare operations continues to evolve, 
competitive advantage will increasingly belong to organizations that can bring domain expertise, decision intelligence, and execution together in one integrated model. Sagility is building and scaling that model for the future of healthcare operations.

=== page 19 ===
Stories of 
the YearFY26 was defined by one of Sagility’s 
strongest financial performances, demonstrating its ability to deliver profitable growth in a challenging business environment. Revenue from operations grew 29.1% to INR 71,929 million  (USD 814 million), while Adjusted EBITDA reached INR 18,200 million with a margin of 25.3%. The performance reflected deepening client relationships, broad-based growth across the portfolio, and rigorous execution. Expansion within existing clients, new client acquisitions, reduced client concentration, and continued momentum in the small and mid-market segments underscore the quality and sustainability of this growth.
The year’s financial performance was 
complemented by meaningful progress across several strategic priorities, including investor engagement, enterprise positioning, market presence, technology transformation, brand evolution, and talent development. These initiatives elevated Sagility’s visibility, relevance, and preparedness for its next phase of growth.
Annual Report 2025-26
4
Sagility Limited Annual Report 2025-26 4
=== page 20 ===
In FY26, Sagility expanded its engagement with the investment community 
through its first Investor and Analyst Meeting in Mumbai. The forum elevated engagement with the financial community, offering a comprehensive view of Sagility’s strategy, differentiated operating model, and long-term value creation priorities. It reflected our commitment to transparency and reinforced confidence in Sagility’s growth trajectory.Deepening Credibility 
with Investors and Financial Analysts
Through FY26, Sagility expanded its industry presence through active participation 
in leading healthcare forums, including AHIP , HFMA Annual Conference, RISE Medicare Marketing & Sales Summit, ILTCI Conference, and OpsIgnite.
These engagements enabled meaningful dialogue with clients, partners, and 
industry leaders, shaping conversations on operational excellence, technology-led transformation, and evolving healthcare models.
Presence across these forums reinforced Sagility’s market visibility and its 
positioning as a trusted, insight-led leader in healthcare operations.Elevating Industry 
Presence and Thought Leadership
FY26 saw Sagility accelerate its technology transformation, further embedding AI 
and digital capabilities into core operations. Building on its deep healthcare domain expertise, Sagility advanced its AI-augmented operating model, enabling greater efficiency, scalability, and decision support across client engagements. 
Investments in proprietary platforms and data-driven workflows have significantly 
enhanced Sagility’s ability to deliver more predictable outcomes, improve productivity, and support clients in navigating increasingly complex healthcare ecosystems. This continued focus on technology-led transformation reinforces Sagility’s position as a future-ready, healthcare operations partner.Advancing Technology 
and AI-Led Transformation
During the year, Sagility undertook comprehensive brand reinvigoration to reflect its 
evolved identity and distinct value in healthcare operations. The refreshed brand draws on what clients, partners, and talent value in Sagility, while also reflecting Sagility’s future direction. Anchored in decades of deep domain expertise, powered by scalable technology and AI, and built on trusted collaboration, the new brand reinforces greater clarity, confidence and credibility in the market.
The reinvigoration introduced a richer, more distinctive design system, including an 
evolved visual identity signalling both the scale of our ambition and the clarity of our purpose. This was complemented by the launch of a revamped company website, enhancing how we communicate our capabilities, solutions, and point of view to global stakeholders.
These efforts create greater coherence across clients, talent, and partners, 
ensuring consistency in how Sagility is understood, experienced, and differentiated in the market, while positioning Sagility for its next phase of growth.Brand Reinvigoration:

Dear Shareholders,
FY26 was an excellent year for Sagility, marked by robust 
growth, improved profitability, and disciplined execution in a rapidly evolving healthcare environment. In an environment shaped by rising medical costs, increasing regulatory complexity, and rapid advances in technology and AI, Sagility delivered strong financial performance while continuing to enhance its long-term competitive position.
Our performance reflects clarity and the focus of our 
strategy. We continue to evolve as a healthcare operations partner that combines deep domain expertise, with technology, analytics and AI to deliver measurable outcomes to clients. Increasingly, our role extends beyond operational execution to accountability for results - strengthening our position in a market that is shifting toward integrated, outcome-driven models.
During the year, we maintained strong financial discipline 
and a clear focus on capital efficiency. Improvements in margins, operational rigor, and balance sheet strength have enhanced our capacity to invest with purpose. These investments are directed toward initiatives that reinforce our competitive advantage and support sustained long-term value creation.
At the same time, we continued to invest in areas that will 
shape the future of healthcare operations. These include AI, platform-led capabilities, and integrated operating models that connect fragmented workflows and enable more outcome-oriented partnerships.  The integration and contribution of BroadPath Healthcare Solutions has extended Sagility’s market reach and complemented its service portfolio, enabling Sagility to address a broader range of client needs with greater flexibility and scale.
Governance remains central to how we operate. In a highly 
regulated industry, sustained success requires both innovation and discipline. We continue to maintain rigorous oversight across risk management, cybersecurity, regulatory compliance, and the responsible deployment of AI and other emerging technologies.
The Board is confident in the leadership team’s ability to 
execute on these priorities. Under the leadership of Ramesh Gopalan, Sagility continues to strengthen its role as a trusted partner to healthcare organizations by bringing together

orchestrated operating systems. During the year, we 
expanded this approach across key internal processes, including hiring, training, quality, coaching, and real-time operations management. By embedding intelligence into how work is planned, executed, monitored, and continuously improved, we are driving greater consistency, accuracy, speed, and control across our operations. Dear Shareholders,
We closed FY26 with revenue of ₹71,929 million (US $814.0 M), 
reflecting a 29.1% year-on-year growth, and 23.6% in constant currency terms. Organic growth excluding BroadPath remained robust at 20.1% in INR terms and 15.0% in constant currency, reflecting sustained momentum across the business. 
Our fourth quarter delivered ₹20,243 million (US $222.1 M) in 
revenue, a 29.1% year-on-year increase in INR terms and 22.2% in constant currency terms. This performance reflects continued demand across both payer and provider markets and strong execution across our operations. The Payer segment accounted for over 89.7% of our revenues and provider segment accounted for 10.3%.
We continued to diversify our revenue base during the year. 
Revenue share from our top three and top five clients declined during the year, resulting in a healthier and more balanced revenue mix. At the end of FY26, we had 82 active client groups, with 17 new clients added during the year.
Our healthcare-focused business model continues to support 
operational excellence, strong client retention and improved operating leverage. This focus, combined with our operational capabilities and delivery excellence, contributed meaningfully to our financial performance during the year. Looking ahead, we remain focused on delivering profitable growth while maintaining financial discipline and operational efficiency.
Adjusted EBITDA for FY26 stood at ₹18,200 million  
(US $206.0 M), representing a robust 23.9% increase (in INR terms) over FY25, with margins at 25.3%.
Adjusted profit after tax grew at an even faster pace, increasing 
39.5% year-on-year to ₹11,306 million reflecting the scalability of our operating model, cost management, and sustained demand from existing clients. Operating cash flow stood at ₹12,030 million, reflecting healthy cash conversion and the quality of our earnings. With adjusted PAT margins increasing to 15.7% from 14.6% last year, we continued to improve efficiency across the business while strengthening our capital structure. 
Revenue growth and margin preservation remain key priorities, 
with cost optimization being a key lever in sustaining our healthy margins. In FY26, we managed wage inflation without compromising profitability. Seasonal demand in the second half of the year was managed effectively, allowing us to scale back excess capacity following the open enrollment season. Total headcount increased by 18.9% during the year, helping support business growth.

The U.S. healthcare industry is undergoing a structural shift in how operations are 
designed and executed. Historically, healthcare operations have relied on fragmented systems, siloed vendors, and effort-intensive workflows. As complexity increases, these models are becoming increasingly difficult to scale efficiently or deliver consistent outcomes.

=== page 33 ===
Administrative 
Complexity Remains a Large OpportunityAdministrative workflows remain one of the largest areas of inefficiency in 
healthcare. Claims processing, prior authorization, payment accuracy, provider operations, and member engagement, each continue to be fragmented, creating operational friction and avoidable costs.
Healthcare organizations are increasingly investing in models that streamline 
workflows, accelerate decision making, and reduce manual intervention. This shift is driving demand for partners who can deliver end-to-end transformation across interconnected workflows rather than optimize isolated processes.
AI Is Becoming  
a Core Operating LayerAI is moving from targeted use cases to broader enterprise adoption across 
healthcare operations.
This progression started with human-led operations consisting largely of manual 
processes managed by people and then moved to AI-enabled operations managed by humans but supported by AI. The current evolution features human operations supported fully by AI workflows and tools.
AI is being applied to areas such as workflow orchestration, decision support, 
document processing, and predictive analytics, improving efficiency and outcomes while maintaining necessary clinical and regulatory controls.
Shift Toward 
Outcomes and Cost OptimizationThe continued expansion of value-based care and rising medical cost pressure are 
accelerating the focus on measurable outcomes.
Health plans and providers are investing in capabilities that improve care 
coordination, utilization management, and quality performance, while also reducing administrative costs. This requires operating models that connect administrative execution with clinical decision-making and financial impact.Evolving  
Consumer ExpectationsMember and patient expectations continue to rise, shaped by experiences in other 
industries. Faster resolution, personalized engagement, and seamless interactions are becoming baseline requirements.
Healthcare organizations are responding by investing in operating models that 
improve engagement across the member and provider lifecycle while reducing friction across complex workflows.

Company Overview
About Sagility
Sagility is the Decision Intelligence and Outcomes layer for 
healthcare operations, focused exclusively on the U.S. healthcare industry. With over 26 years of deep domain expertise, Sagility partners with payers, providers, and pharmacy benefit managers to manage and improve mission-critical operations across administrative, financial, and clinical workflows.
Sagility delivers end-to-end capabilities across the 
healthcare value chain, including member and provider lifecycle management, claims operations, clinical utilization and care management, payment integrity, and provider revenue cycle management. These capabilities combine operational scale with deep clinical and coding expertise to improve quality, compliance, and cost outcomes.Sagility operates through long-standing, deeply embedded client relationships, reflected in high retention rates and sustained engagement across leading healthcare organizations. Sagility serves more than 80 healthcare client enterprises, including several of the largest health plans and pharmacy benefit managers in the United States.
With a global delivery footprint spanning the United States, 
India, the Philippines, Jamaica, and Colombia, and a workforce of over 46,000 professionals, including clinicians and technology specialists, Sagility delivers services at scale while maintaining close alignment with client objectives.
Over time, Sagility has evolved its operating model through the 
application of AI and automation, moving from AI-enabled to AI-embedded operations. This approach enables intelligence to be applied directly within workflows, improving accuracy, consistency, and performance across complex healthcare processes.

Practice and Solutions
Practice Overview
Sagility’s Practices are designed to manage and improve critical healthcare operations across the payer and provider 
ecosystem. These capabilities span the full healthcare value chain, combining deep domain expertise with operational execution to deliver measurable outcomes.
The Practice portfolio is organized across six core areas:
These Practices are further advanced through consulting and technology services, including assessments, transformation 
programs, platform enablement, and AI-led orchestration.
This integrated portfolio enables Sagility to move beyond isolated process execution toward managing connected workflows and 
delivering measurable outcomes across healthcare operations.Member Lifecycle Management
Supports the end-to-end member journey, including enrollment, billing, reconciliation, benefits management and ongoing engagement. This helps payers improve accuracy and timeliness, reduces revenue leakage and improves member experience.Clinical Utilization and Care 
Management (UM/CM)
Includes utilization management, case management, 
disease programs, and quality initiatives. These services deliver better care coordination and improved clinical and financial outcomes for our payer and provider clients.
Provider Lifecycle Management
Covers provider operations such as credentialing, contract management, data maintenance, and engagement. These capabilities help payers maintain accurate provider networks improving access to care for the members and reducing downstream operational friction.Payment Integrity (PI)
Focuses on identifying, preventing, and recovering inappropriate payments across the claims lifecycle. This helps payers to reduce leakage and better financial control.
Claims Management
Encompasses intake, adjudication support, rework, and appeals. These services enable payers to improve claims accuracy, reduce turnaround times, enhance operational efficiency and improve provider and member engagement.Provider Revenue Cycle Management 
(RCM)
Supports Providers across billing, coding, denial 
management, and collections to improve revenue realization and cash flow performance.

Managed Services Model
Sagility’s Managed Services model is designed to help healthcare 
organizations move beyond fragmented outsourcing relationships toward integrated, outcome-driven operating models.
The approach begins with a structured assessment of operational 
performance across critical workflows such as enrollment, claims administration, care management, provider operations, and payment processes. This assessment identifies inefficiencies, cost leakage, and opportunities for standardization, automation, and improved decision-making.
Based on these insights, Sagility deploys an integrated operating 
model that combines domain expertise, operational execution, technology, and AI to deliver measurable business outcomes. The focus is on redesigning workflows and embedding intelligence directly into day-to-day operations to improve accuracy, speed, and consistency.
This emphasis includes capabilities such as workflow 
transformation, automation, analytics, clinical decision support, and the orchestration of interconnected processes across the healthcare value chain. These elements are applied selectively to drive tangible impact, including cost reduction, improved quality, faster turnaround times, and enhanced member and  
provider experiences.
A key differentiator of this model is accountability for outcomes. 
Engagements are increasingly structured around performance, aligning Sagility’s success directly with client results. This shifts the relationship from transactional execution to shared ownership of business outcomes.
The model enables clients to simplify vendor ecosystems, 
modernize legacy workflows, and transition from effort-based operating approaches to more scalable,  
outcome-oriented models.Medical Loss Ratio (MLR) 
Reduction Solutions
Sagility’s MLR Reduction Solutions are designed to help 
healthcare organizations manage and reduce medical costs while improving quality outcomes across the  care continuum.
These solutions focus on high-impact areas such as 
utilization management, care coordination, clinical review, and quality improvement programs. In these applications, the use of analytics and AI, which enables risk scoring,  more accurate clinical decision-making, and better intervention timing, significantly influences medical cost and health outcomes, with the same or lower operational spend.
The approach integrates Sagility’s domain expertise, data 
scientists specializing in payer-clinical data analytics, at-scale clinical services, operational excellence, and technology to improve precision in key workflows, including prior authorization, case management, and ongoing utilization monitoring. By embedding intelligence into these processes, Sagility enables more consistent application of clinical guidelines, earlier identification of risk, and proactive intervention.

Sagility’s technology capabilities have evolved through a deliberate progression 
from process optimization to orchestrating end-to-end healthcare operations  at scale.
The journey began with process improvement, where standardization and 
operational discipline strengthened core workflows. It then evolved into technology enablement, leveraging automation, analytics, and process visibility tools to improve efficiency and consistency.
Building on this foundation, Sagility embedded GenAI-augmented operations, 
integrating healthcare-specific models into workflows to accelerate knowledge access and improve execution quality. This has further evolved into agentic AI-embedded operations, where multi-agent orchestration and human-in-the-loop governance enable more adaptive and responsive execution.
Today, this progression is advancing toward Lifecycle Operations, enabled through 
Sagility Synchrony across domains such as Medicare, utilization management, claims, and payment integrity. At the Lifecycle Operations stage, intelligence is applied continuously across workflows, improving decision speed, bolstering accuracy, and enabling more consistent outcomes at scale.

Sagility’s delivery model is built to operate at scale, ensure 
consistency across geographies, and maintain rigorous governance in complex healthcare environments. 
Sagility operates a globally distributed delivery model across 
the United States, India, the Philippines, Jamaica, and Colombia, anchored by 31 delivery centres. This footprint is optimized continuously to expand to Tier 2 locations to unlock cost advantage and deepen talent access.
A hybrid delivery model enables more than 50% of associates  
to work-at-home (WAH). This materially enhances agility, broadens the addressable talent pool, and builds greater resiliency through geographic diversification.
The model is designed to flex with client demand, supporting 
both steady-state operations and large-scale ramp-ups across enrollment cycles, claims processing, and clinical workflows.
Delivery is sustained by a meticulous quality and compliance 
framework aligned to global standards including ISO 27001, URAC, SOC 1 and SOC 2, HiTrust, HIPAA, PCI-DSS, and NCQA. Embedded into how we operate, these benchmarks tailored to meet the unique needs of each service line, ensuring uncompromising security, reliability, and regulatory adherence.

Enterprise Transformation (ET) is a strategic 
enterprise function that accelerates business transformation by integrating AI, enterprise operating model modernization, brand and market strategy, corporate communications, and go-to-market enablement — ensuring Sagility evolves how it operates, engages the market, and delivers value to clients.
ET builds and scales a connected portfolio of 
SmarTec Workmates, role-aligned AI coworkers that augment human capability, accelerate execution, and enable consistent, outcome-driven performance across the enterprise. They operate on a shared architectural backbone, common data foundations, and unified governance, ensuring scalability, reuse, and disciplined adoption across functions. 
Not a one-time initiative, Enterprise 
Transformation is a standing capability with end-to-end accountability across the lifecycle of each workmate, from vision and design to build, validation, pilot, scale, and value realization. The mandate is to ensure that every workmate is purpose-built for outcomes, operationally embedded, and continuously refined through structured feedback and adoption.AI-augmented

Sagility’s talent strategy is focused on building 
a future-ready workforce that can support its shift toward a more technology and AI-led, outcome-driven operating model. As this transition progresses, the organization is evolving toward a human-in-the-loop approach that combines domain expertise with technology to improve productivity and decision-making.
Sagility is operationalizing a skills-first talent 
model that is supported by more data-driven hiring and workforce planning. This approach enables faster talent deployment consistent with evolving business priorities.
As of March 31, 2026, Sagility’s workforce 
stood at 46,860 employees, reflecting steady investment in talent aligned with business growth and delivery requirements. A specialized pool of over 4,400 clinicians, allied health professionals, nurses, coders, and technologists, now augments Sagility’s ability to deliver high-quality, domain-intensive healthcare services.
Sagility continues to leverage its global 
delivery footprint to scale operations efficiently while supporting fluctuating client needs, including seasonal and member-facing workloads. Attrition trends have remained within reasonable limits and are being actively managed through focused retention initiatives, career development opportunities, and investment in employee engagement.
Talent development is actively encouraged 
through a personalized learning approach supported by technology-enabled platforms and structured capability-building initiatives. These include dedicated Healthcare and Digital & AI Academies, to deepen domain expertise and build digital fluency across the workforce.

Sagility’s acquisition strategy is designed to 
advance its long-term growth objectives by building differentiated healthcare capabilities, expanding technology-enabled solutions, and increasing market reach in attractive segments. Sagility pursues acquisitions that deepen domain expertise, accelerate platform-led innovation, and enhance its ability to deliver integrated, AI-enabled healthcare operations.
Potential acquisitions are evaluated based on 
their strategic alignment with our healthcare focus, technology-enabled operating model, and long-term growth priorities. Sagility emphasizes opportunities that complement existing capabilities, create meaningful revenue and operational synergies, expand client relationships, and can be integrated effectively while maintaining service quality, cultural alignment, and financial discipline.
In recent years, Sagility has executed targeted 
acquisitions to deepen its healthcare capabilities to achieve leadership positioning in its practice areas. In April 2023, Sagility acquired Devlin Consulting for its differentiated Contract
-Central 
cl
aims repricing platform, proven with large 
national plans, to enhance its payment integrity offerings through secondary claims adjudication and post
-pa
yment recovery solutions for 
healthcare payers. Since acquisition, the business has more than doubled, supported by an expanding scope of services to include data mining services and adding  overpayment recovery capabilities. In March 2024, Sagility acquired BirchAI, a generative AI platform provider powered by transformer based natural language processing, to enhance AI enabled member engagement capabilities and automate domain intensive service operations across claims and clinical processes. 
In January 2025, Sagility acquired BroadPath, a 
healthcare-focused services provider, expanding its presence in the small and mid-market payer segment and adding more than 30 healthcare clients. The acquisition strengthened Sagility’s relationships with leading U.S. health plans, broadened its market reach, and created opportunities to cross-sell its technology-enabled healthcare operations and transformation services across an expanded client base.
The acquisition also added new member 
enrollment capabilities and a scalable remote delivery model enabled by a proprietary productivity enhancement workforce management platform called BHive.
Through such acquisitions, the organization 
seeks to augment organic growth, deepen client relationships, and reinforce its competitive positioning across core and adjacent healthcare segments. Sagility continues to assess inorganic growth opportunities through proactive market intelligence and well-established banker network in a disciplined manner, with a focus on long-term value creation, effective integration, and consistency with its overall strategic and financial objectives.

INDUSTRY OVERVIEW
The United States healthcare market remains the largest and 
among the most complex in the world, shaped by sustained demand, expanding coverage needs, rising clinical and administrative intensity, and continued pressure to improve affordability, access, and outcomes. According to the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services, national health expenditure is projected to reach 20.3% of GDP by 2033, up from 17.6% in 2023, underscoring both the scale of the market and the urgency of improving how healthcare is administered, managed, and delivered.
Despite this scale, the U.S. healthcare system continues to 
face persistent inefficiencies. Administrative fragmentation, disconnected workflows, rising operating costs, and uneven consumer and patient experiences continue to create friction across the ecosystem. These challenges are intensifying as payers and providers manage higher volumes, more complex member and patient journeys, and increasing expectations for speed, accuracy, responsiveness, and personalization.
STRUCTURAL DRIVERS OF DEMAND AND 
TRANSFORMATION OF CARE DELIVERY
The healthcare industry continues to be shaped by several 
long-duration forces that are increasing both demand and complexity, while also changing how care is financed, delivered, and experienced.
One of the most significant of these is demographic change. 
The U.S. Census Bureau has identified 2030 as a demographic turning point, when all baby boomers will be over age 65 and one in five U.S. residents will be of retirement age. Older populations typically require more frequent and more complex healthcare interactions and are more likely to live with multiple Management Discussion and Analysis
chronic conditions, increasing the need for coordinated care, 
longitudinal engagement, utilization management, and more sophisticated operational support.
The burden of chronic disease remains another defining driver. 
Diabetes, cardiovascular conditions, respiratory illness, and other long-duration conditions continue to account for a significant share of utilization and healthcare spending. This is reinforcing the importance of prevention, risk stratification, care coordination, and population-level interventions across the healthcare ecosystem.
Coverage dynamics are adding further complexity across the 
healthcare ecosystem.  While Medicare Advantage
1 enrolment 
continues to increase, growth is maturing and profitability pressures are intensifying. Plans are responding by investing in AI, automation, operational efficiency, Stars performance, utilization management, payment integrity, and member experience to protect margins while managing an increasingly complex and highly regulated population. At the same time, Medicaid programs are undergoing significant change as states continue post-pandemic eligibility redeterminations while preparing for funding, eligibility, and operational changes stemming from the One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBB). In parallel, the expiration of enhanced Affordable Care Act Marketplace premium subsidies and new marketplace enrollment requirements are expected to drive shifts in Exchange enrollment and member affordability. Together, these policy changes are increasing membership volatility across Medicaid and Exchange populations, requiring health plans to strengthen capabilities across enrollment, eligibility, claims, billing, payment integrity, and member engagement while maintaining operational precision in an increasingly complex regulatory environment.
At the same time, consumer expectations are changing. 
Patients and members increasingly expect convenience, transparency, speed, and personalization in healthcare interactions. The growth of digital channels and self-service expectations is accelerating the need for engagement models that are more responsive, experience-led, and integrated across the healthcare journey.
Care delivery itself is also evolving. The industry continues to 
move toward value-based models that place greater emphasis on outcomes, quality, and cost efficiency. In parallel, care is increasingly shifting toward lower-cost and more accessible settings, including outpatient, ambulatory, virtual, and home-based environments. These transitions are increasing the need for stronger coordination across administrative, clinical, and financial workflows.
These structural drivers underscore the need for operating 
models that are integrated, scalable, and capable of performing consistently in complex healthcare environments.

The competitive landscape is also evolving. Healthcare 
organizations are moving away from fragmented vendor relationships and isolated point solutions toward more integrated partners that can combine domain expertize, workflow transformation, analytics, AI enablement, and accountable execution. In parallel, technology-native firms, platform providers, and diversified services players are increasing their presence across the ecosystem, raising the standard for speed, interoperability, and measurable outcomes. These factors are shifting the basis of competition from scale alone to relevance, integration, and the ability to deliver sustained value across end-to-end workflows.
Sagility’s strategy is aligned to these shifts. We continue 
to deepen our capabilities across payer and provider workflows, including enrolment, claims, payment integrity, care management, revenue cycle support, and engagement operations, in order to help clients navigate rising complexity with greater precision, efficiency, and accountability.
We believe the next phase of growth in healthcare operations 
will be defined by the ability to combine domain expertize, decision intelligence, and execution into a single operating model and translate that into measurable outcomes.
Against this backdrop, our priorities are focused and consistent.
• Scaling technology and AI-led, outcome-based 
engagements across our client base, aligning more 
directly with the business impact we deliver
• Expanding connected lifecycle operating models, 
including Synchrony, across administrative, clinical, and financial operations.
At the same time, Sagility is expanding into the mid- and small-
market segment , leveraging our BroadPath acquisition. We will 
continue to invest in capabilities that strengthen our long-term 
structural advantage, while pursuing targeted acquisitions  
that deepen our domain expertize, expand our technology and AI capabilities, and reinforce our positioning in the market.
In FY 2027, we will scale with greater emphasis on execution 
consistency, client outcomes and operational discipline.
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE AS A CORE 
TRANSFORMATION LAYER
Artificial intelligence is rapidly emerging as a foundational 
transformation layer across healthcare operations. Its value lies not only in automation, but in improving how decisions are made, how workflows are orchestrated, and how complex administrative and clinical support processes are executed with greater speed, consistency, and intelligence. In a sector defined by fragmentation, regulatory complexity, and high consequence decisions, AI is increasingly becoming central to how healthcare organizations improve performance while managing risk.The next wave of impact will come from embedding intelligence 
directly into operational workflows rather than treating AI as a standalone tool. This includes intelligent triage, guided decision support, workflow prioritization, document and data interpretation, exception management, and agent-assisted execution across areas such as claims, payment integrity, care management, revenue cycle, contact centre operations, and provider enablement. As these capabilities mature, organizations will increasingly shift from isolated pilots toward enterprize-scale deployment that delivers measurable business outcomes.
Healthcare, is not an environment where generic automation 
alone is sufficient. The operating context requires domain specificity, regulatory awareness, auditability, and the ability to combine machine intelligence with human judgment. AI adoption in this sector therefore favours models that are deeply embedded within healthcare workflows and supported by clinical, operational, and compliance expertize. This creates significant differentiation for organizations that can responsibly operationalize AI in a way that improves cost, quality, experience, and performance without compromising control.
For Sagility, AI is not a parallel capability. It is increasingly 
embedded into the operating model itself. Our approach combines healthcare domain expertize, workflow understanding, platform enablement, and human-in-the-loop execution so that intelligence is applied where it can improve decisions and outcomes. We believe this will define and 
transform the next generation of healthcare operations.
Driving ROI from AI Through Domain-Led Execution: Sagility’s 
approach to AI is fundamentally anchored in domain strategy rather than a platform-led lens. Sagility focuses on embedding AI into healthcare workflows where impact can be directly measured, moving beyond isolated automation to driving meaningful business outcomes.
SAGILITY’S UNIQUE POSITIONING – DELIVERING 
DIFFERENTIATED IMPACT
We operate at the intersection of healthcare domain 
depth, operational execution, and technology and AI-led transformation. Our positioning is shaped by the needs of a market that increasingly demands more than capacity augmentation or fragmented point solutions. Clients are looking for partners who understand the complexity of healthcare workflows, can execute at scale, and can translate technology and AI into measurable business outcomes. We believe Sagility is well positioned to play that role.
Our differentiation begins with deep specialization in 
healthcare. We serve complex payer and provider workflows where accuracy, regulatory discipline, and operating precision matter. This domain intensity allows us to engage in areas that are difficult to commoditize and increasingly important to clients, including payment integrity, utilization management, care support, enrolment and billing, revenue cycle operations, and member and patient engagement. It also creates the foundation for more effective deployment of AI, analytics, and

platform-led solutions because the intelligence is grounded in 
healthcare context rather than applied generically.
We are also evolving beyond traditional delivery constructs

Through integrated workflow orchestration, platform enablement, AI-augmented execution, and outcomes-based engagement structures, we are helping clients move from siloed operations to more intelligent and accountable models of performance. This reflects The Company’s broader ambition to serve as the decision intelligence and outcomes layer for healthcare operations.
Sagility’s market opportunity is reinforced by the way the 
ecosystem itself is changing. As payers, providers, and adjacent healthcare stakeholders pursue tighter integration, platform-based coordination, and greater accountability for outcomes, the demand for operating partners with deep healthcare specialization and transformation capability is increasing. We believe this favours organizations that can combine execution depth with workflow redesign, intelligence, and scalable delivery.
ENTERING FY 2027: SCALING WHAT WORKS
As Sagility enters FY 2027, the focus will remain on scaling the capabilities and engagement models that are already demonstrating relevance in the market. This includes expanding technology and AI-led and platform-enabled solutions, deepening outcome-based partnerships, extending integrated lifecycle models such as Synchrony, and broadening our reach across client segments through assets such as BroadPath. We will continue to invest in the capabilities that strengthen long-term differentiation, while remaining disciplined in how we allocate capital and prioritize growth.Sagility’s objective is not simply to grow with the market, but to 
help shape the next model of healthcare operations through deeper integration of expertize, intelligence, and execution. This focus positions Sagility to create sustained impact for clients while strengthening the resilience and quality of our own growth trajectory.
Looking ahead, Sagility expects the market to continue shifting 
toward AI-enabled, platform-supported, and more integrated operating models. At the same time, clients will place greater emphasis on partners that can help them manage complexity, improve experience, reduce administrative waste, and create measurable financial and operational outcomes at scale.
FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE 
FY 2026 marked a year of robust growth and improved 
profitability despite a challenging and evolving healthcare environment. We delivered robust performance, reflecting disciplined execution, deep client relevance, and the resilience of its operating model.
Revenue from operations for the year stood at ` 71,929 Million 
(US$ 814 Million), representing year-on-year growth of 29.1% in ` terms and 23.6% in constant currency. This growth was 
broad-based, driven by continued expansion within existing relationships as well as momentum in new client acquisition. The Company’s top 5 clients grew 16.7% year-on-year, underscoring our ability to advance our relationships and scale engagements in complex, high-value workflows. 
Concentration in the top clients reduced in FY26 as we 
expanded our portfolio with new clients and advanced our relationship with smaller, yet important, existing clients. The expanded presence of small and mid-segment accounts has created resilience and balance in Sagility’s revenue profile over time. Sagility added 17 new clients during the year,

Revenue from Operations 
Revenue from operations increased by 29.1% to `71,929 Million for FY 2026 from ` 55,699 Million for FY 2025. This increase was due 
to growth from existing clients, new deal wins and full year impact of acquisition of the of BroadPath.
Revenue growth in reported terms include the impact of currency fluctuations. We, therefore, additionally report the revenue growth 
in constant currency terms. Our Revenues from Operations in FY 2026 in constant currency terms was US $814.0 Million a 23.6% increase over FY 2025 (US $658.3 M).
Further, Sagility also added 17 new clients in the FY 2026, which also contributed to the increase in our revenue from operations.Revenue from our payer clients increased by 29.6% while revenue from our provider clients increased by 25.6%. Revenue from 
operations from our top five clients increased by 16.7% to ` 50,606 Million for FY 2026 from ` 43,370 Million for FY 2025. Below is a

Other income primarily comprises net foreign exchange

Employee Benefit Expenses
Employee benefit expenses excluding share-based payment 
awards increased to 62.5% of revenue in FY 2026 from 60.8% in FY 2025. This increase is attributed to higher onshore mix and full-year impact of BroadPath acquisition.
Employee benefit expenses including share-based payment 
awards as a percentage of revenue stood at 62.7% in FY 2026 vs 62.8% in FY 2025. 
Other Expenses
Other expenses primarily consist of legal and professional fees, 
software subscription charges, travel, communication, power and fuel, marketing, recruitment and short-term rent expenses etc. 
In FY 2026, other expenses decreased to 12.8% from 13.9% 
in FY 2025. Decrease is majorly on account of operational improvements and favourable foreign exchange movements. 
Finance Costs
Finance costs include interest on promoter debt and lease 
liabilities. The expense decreased as the borrowings came down because of repayment of promoter debt.
Depreciation and Amortization
Depreciation and amortization expense increased marginally to 
` 4,874 Million in March 2026 from ` 4,668 Million in Mar 2025.The increase is primarily attributable to full year impact of 
the amortization of intangible assets created in relation to acquisition of BroadPath.
Exceptional Item
Exceptional Items represents one time impact of employee 
benefit obligations arising from the “New Labour Codes” introduced by Government of India effective 21
st November 
2025.
Sagility has undertaken salary restructuring exercise in 
compliance with the New Labour Codes which has resulted in an increase in the provision for gratuity and leave encashment.
Taxes 
Tax expenses for FY 2026 is ` 3,141 Million (FY 2025: ` 2,211 
Million). Increase is tax expense is primarily attributable to 
higher profits netted-off with lower books to tax adjustment. FY 2025, had higher books to tax adjustment on account of one-time increase in the fair value of share-based payment awards which was a non-deductible expense for income tax purposes.

RISK MANAGEMENT 
Sagility’s Board of Directors has overall responsibility for the 
establishment and oversight of Sagility’s risk management framework. The Company’s risk management policies are established to identify and analyse the risks faced by The Company, to set appropriate risk limits and controls and to monitor and mitigate risks. Risk management policies and systems are reviewed regularly to reflect changes in market conditions and The Company’s activities. 
Sagility has a Risk Management Committee chaired by an 
Independent Director, which assists the Board in monitoring and overseeing implementation of the risk management policy, including evaluating the adequacy of risk management systems and such other functions as mandated under the SEBI Listing Regulations and as the Board may deem fit from time to time. The composition, detailed terms of reference of the Committee and attendance at its meetings are provided as part of the Corporate Governance Report. 
Sagility’s Board monitors compliance with The Company 
risk management policies and procedures and reviews the adequacy of the risk management framework in relation to the risks faced by The Company. At present, in the opinion of the Board of Directors, there are no risks which may threaten the existence of The Company. 
The Risk Management policy is available on the website of 
Sagility at https://Sagility.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Sagility-risk-management-policy.pdf .

RISK MITIGATION SUMMARY
Risk Theme Description Mitigation
Geo-Political 
and Sector Risks• Sagility derives its entire revenue 
from the U.S. healthcare industry, exposing it to regulatory, economic, and outsourcing-related developments in that market.• To mitigate these risks, we are continuing to deepen and diversify our 
presence across the U.S. healthcare ecosystem by serving both Payer and Provider segments. In parallel, we are expanding into adjacent, higher-value areas such as clinical services and pharmacy benefit management, strengthening our ability to support clients across a broader range of healthcare operations.
• In addition, continued focus on regulatory compliance, information 
security, delivery resilience and client-specific operating models helps Sagility respond effectively to healthcare policy, market and outsourcing-related changes.
• Sagility operates delivery centres across multiple geographies, 
including onshore and nearshore locations in the U.S., Jamaica and Colombia, as well as offshore centres in India and the Philippines. This geographically diversified delivery footprint reduces dependence on any single country and helps mitigate country-specific political, regulatory, talent and business-continuity risks.
• Sagility has negligible dependency on H1B visas issued by the U.S. 
Government. Throughout the year, it actively monitors U.S. political and legislative developments including proposed measures such as the HIRE Act and state-level anti-outsourcing bills, through its dedicated tax and legal & compliance teams, as well as third-party consultants. For any high-impact legislative changes that could affect operations, Sagility evaluates potential implications and develops appropriate response strategies to ensure business continuity and compliance.
Client Concentration and Client Restructuring Risks
• A significant portion of rev enue is 
concentrated among a few large clients, with the top 10 client groups contributing 83.9% of total revenue.
• Client overlap s within the sector, 
including consolidation, mergers, acquisitions or restructuring among healthcare payers and providers, may result in vendor rationalization, contract renegotiation, volume reallocation or pricing pressure. • Sagility is actively diversifying its revenue base by strengthening 
capabilities across both, payer and provider segments. Within payers, Sagility is expanding into higher-value clinical services and patient support offerings for pharmacy benefit managers and specialty pharmacy players. Within the provider segment, it continues to scale end-to-end Revenue Cycle Management capabilities.
• While the payer segment remains largely consolidated, Sagility serves 
7 of the Top 10 payers. Sagility maintains long-standing relationships with leading payers, supported by multi-year contracts, performance-linked contracts that provide revenue visibility. These relationships are further reinforced through deep operational integration, multiple stakeholder touchpoints, and long-standing engagement models.
• Acquisition of BroadPath represents a strategic step towards entering 
the mid and small-market segment, and broadening the client base and service portfolio. In parallel, Sagility is increasing integration within client workflows through proprietary platforms, strengthening client stickiness and increasing switching costs.
• Sagility maintains a proactive approach to restructuring risks, 
including continuous monitoring of client-specific developments and industry signals. During periods of consolidation or M&A, it actively engages with clients to ensure transition continuity and protect revenue streams.

Risk Theme Description Mitigation
Risk of 
Fluctuations in Client Business Volumes• Sagility’s revenues are linked to 
client-driven transaction volumes across payer and provider operations. These volumes may fluctuate due to factors such as membership trends, cost pressures, regulatory changes, outsourcing decisions, or strategic reprioritization by clients. 
• Risk of insour cing of the work by the 
clients
• Such variability may affect revenue 
predictability, capacity utilization, and operating leverage.• Sagility mitigates volume variability through a combination of 
diversified revenue streams, flexible operating models, and contract structures aligned to client demand. 
• Multi-year engagements and outcome-based commercial constructs 
provide revenue visibility, while expansion across clients, service lines, and segments reduces dependence on any single volume driver. 
• Sagility also maintains operational flexibility through dynamic 
workforce management, cross-trained talent pools, and multi-geo, scalable delivery infrastructure, enabling efficient alignment of capacity with demand. 
• In addition, continuous client engagement and pipeline visibility 
support proactive planning and early response to volume changes. 
• Together, these measures strengthen operational integration and 
client dependence on Sagility’s platforms and delivery model, reducing the likelihood of insourcing and supporting long-term engagement stability.
Competition Risk
• Sagility operates in an evolving 
market, with competition from global outsourcing providers, broad-based IT and business services firms, as well as specialized healthcare-focused companies. 
• In addition, clients continue to 
enhance in-house capabilities and explore digital-led operating models, further intensifying competitive dynamics.
• This competitiv e intensity, along 
with clients continued focus on cost efficiency, results in evolving commercial constructs and pricing dynamics. • Sagility differentiates through an integrated, domain-led operating 
model that combines deep healthcare expertise with technology, analytics, and platform-led delivery. It continues to shift toward outcome-based and managed services constructs that align more closely with client objectives, enabling value-led engagements rather than price-led competition.
• Competitivene ss in bids is enhanced through structured deals that 
prioritize multi-year value creation over short-term returns, along with a continued expansion of non-linear, output-based commercial models. Expansion into higher-value clinical and adjacent service lines, along with platform integration within client workflows, further strengthens differentiation and increases switching barriers.
• In parallel, Sagility leverages proactive geo-shifts and a multi-
shore delivery model to optimize cost structures and enhance competitiveness, while long-standing client relationships and cross-sell opportunities support deeper engagement and sustained growth.
• Financial discipline is maintained through regular monitoring of 
account margins, with periodic reporting to leadership ensuring transparency and sustained profitability.
Cybersecurity and Information Security
• Sagility handles sensitive health 
information and is exposed to risks arising from potential data breaches.
• Non-compliance with stringent 
U.S. data protection regulations, including HIPAA, HITECH, and CCPA, may result in regulatory action and reputational impact.• Sagility has established a robust cybersecurity framework, supported 
by advanced security capabilities including encryption, identity management, access controls, and network protection systems to safeguard sensitive data.
• Cybersecurity controls are continuously strengthened through 
periodic risk assessments, vulnerability testing, and independent external reviews.
• Ongoing training and awareness programs help embed security 
awareness across the organization.
• Sagility also maintains market-benchmarked cyber insurance 
coverage to mitigate potential financial exposure arising from cyber incidents.

Risk Theme Description Mitigation
Talent 
Management and Attrition (Including Succession Planning)• Elevated attrition and continued

• Sagility operates in a competitive 
talent market, which may affect its ability to attract and retain skilled professionals across key roles.

benefits programs to attract and retain talent.
• Workforce capability is strengthened through structured hiring, 
training, and continuous skill development initiatives.
The Company continues to invest in employee engagement through ongoing programs and periodic satisfaction surveys, enabling early identification of improvement areas.

processes, with defined pipelines and structured talent mapping for key roles. These are supported by detailed Talent Cards capturing performance, aspirations, mobility, and development priorities.
ESG
• Environmental, Social and 
Governance (ESG) considerations continue to gain importance, with increasing expectations from regulators, investors, and clients around sustainable, responsible, and ethical business practices.
• Sagility’s ability to effectively 
manage ESG priorities remains important to meeting stakeholder expectations and supporting long-term value creation.• Sagility has established a comprehensive ESG management 
framework aligned with global reporting standards, enabling structured monitoring and continuous improvement across operations. ESG considerations are embedded into business processes and decision-making, supporting alignment with evolving stakeholder expectations.
• Governance and oversight are strengthened through Board-level 
supervision, including a dedicated Corporate Social Responsibility and Sustainability Committees. 
• Sagility has adopted Business Responsibility and Sustainability 
Reporting (BRSR), a recognized ESG disclosure framework in India applicable to India’s top 1,000 listed companies (by market capitalization).  
Inability to Scale Technology / AI Adoption
• Clients across payer and provider 
segments are increasingly prioritizing measurable outcomes in areas such as cost optimization, automation, and member engagement, supported by technology-led solutions.
• Sagility’s ability to effectively 
scale AI-led solutions - while ensuring accuracy, safety, and compliance, remains important to delivering consistent outcomes and sustaining competitiveness.
• Rapid advancements in technology 
also require continuous evolution of platforms and tools to remain relevant and aligned with client expectations.• Sagility’s technology strategy is anchored in a structured roadmap 
aligned to evolving industry needs, with defined milestones and disciplined execution. Capability building is further accelerated through targeted acquisitions focused on AI and emerging technologies.
• A dedicated Go-to-Market Technology function works closely with 
Business Excellence and Delivery teams to design, deploy, and scale tech- & AI-led solutions across workflows. This is complemented by strategic partnerships that combine platform capabilities with Sagility’s domain expertize.
• Continuous monit oring of client technology priorities and adoption 
trends enables early identification of shifts and timely alignment of solutions.
• Investments in proprietary platforms and AI capabilities, including 
BirchAI, healthcare-focused LLMs, and automation-led solutions, are strengthening Sagility’s ability to deliver scalable, outcome-driven services.
• Ongoing focus on responsible AI deployment, including governance, 
testing, and guardrails, supports reliable and compliant adoption at scale.
